The dephasing channel collapses a quantum state into a given classical basis, losing its
essential quantum properties

. Yet it transmits states of a preferential orthonormal

basis without error, making it noiseless for classical information

. This is the crucial

structural fact: the channel is thermodynamically irreversible, classically viable, and
quantum sterile.
This means that classical information is not a degraded form of quantum information. It
is an autonomous layer with its own dynamics, its own arrow of time, and its own viability
conditions. The classical world is not merely the quantum world viewed through a
coarse-grained lens; it is a distinct stratum with its own structural integrity.

Implications for "It from Bit"
The hierarchy forces a revision of the "it from bit" hypothesis in three specific ways.
First, the hypothesis must be stratified. The original claim—"every it derives its
existence from bits"—conflates three distinct informational layers. The dephasing
channel shows that "it" (the classical reality of populations) can exist without "bit"
(quantum coherence). The corrected claim is:
Classical "it" derives from classical bits (populations, diagonal elements).
Quantum "it" derives from quantum bits (coherences, off-diagonal elements).
The two are independent: classical "it" can exist without quantum "bit," and quantum
"bit" can exist without a definite classical "it" (entanglement without a definite state).
This stratification is not a weakening of Wheeler's programme; it is a refinement that
makes it operationally precise. The reconstruction programme of the last two decades—
Hardy, Chiribella–D'Ariano–Perinotti, Masanes–Müller, Zurek—has already moved in this
direction by deriving quantum structure from operational principles 2 . The hierarchy
provides the arrow-theoretic dimension of this reconstruction: quantum theory is not
derived from classical information, nor classical from quantum, but both are derived
from the requirement of stable record-keeping in the presence of noise 2 .
Second, the quantum arrow is a refinement, not a foundation. The coherent information
Q is bounded above by the spectral gap Δ (Q ≤ Δ), with equality only for channels that
are both non-dissipative and non-dephasing. The dephasing channel is the extreme
case where the bound is loose: Δ > 0 while Q = 0. This means that the thermodynamic
arrow is more fundamental than the quantum arrow—it exists whenever there is any
information loss, while the quantum arrow requires additional conditions (preservation of
coherence).

In the "it from bit" framework, this inverts the usual ordering. The quantum arrow is not
the fundamental arrow from which the thermodynamic arrow emerges; rather, the
thermodynamic arrow is the primitive, and the quantum arrow is a refinement that
appears only when the channel preserves coherence. The "bit" (quantum information) is
not the foundation; it is a special case of the thermodynamic arrow where the lost
information happens to be quantum.
Third, the classical arrow is the operational interface. The classical mutual information
is the measure of what can be reliably transmitted through the channel, regardless of its
quantum capacity. The dephasing channel has full classical capacity despite zero
quantum capacity. This means that classical information is the operational interface
between the channel and the external world—it is what an observer can extract without
needing quantum resources.
In Wheeler's terms, "it" (the classical reality accessible to observation) is built from
classical bits, not quantum bits. The observer's "it" is the classical shadow of the
quantum "bit." The dephasing channel is the minimal example of a system where the
shadow is present but the bit is gone.

The Structural Picture
The hierarchy suggests a structural picture that is neither "it from bit" nor "bit from it,"
but "it and bit from record-keeping." The reconstruction programme has already
articulated this: a world required to hold stable, re-readable, copyable facts in the
presence of noise is pushed step by step into the machinery of complex, errorcorrecting quantum mechanics 2 . The thermodynamic arrow is the cost of this recordkeeping; the classical arrow is the content of the records; the quantum arrow is the
coherence that survives the noise.
This is a process-theoretic rather than a reductionist ontology. The fundamental entity is
not the bit or the qubit, but the record—the stable, reproducible correlation that survives
noise. Wheeler's "it" is the record's content; his "bit" is the record's form. The two are
co-constitutive, not reducible to one another.

Conclusion
The dephasing channel's degeneracy is not a problem for the "it from bit" hypothesis; it
is a clarification. It shows that the hypothesis, taken literally and reductionistically, is
false. But it also shows that a stratified, process-theoretic version of the hypothesis is
precisely correct: classical reality, thermodynamic irreversibility, and quantum
coherence are three independent strata of a single record-keeping process, each with
its own arrow of time and its own operational measure. The correct slogan is not "it from
bit" or "bit from it," but "it and bit from record."
